The driver stopped measuring the door.","\"I was hoping for an autograph request,\" she said.","The man reached inside his coat. Mara threw the clipboard at his knees and ran. She had no intention of finding out whether he carried a weapon, a telephone, or an especially disappointing photograph of her.","Behind the loading bay, a narrow stair led to the old observation platform. She took it because the men were between her and the dock, and because a restoration apprentice learns early that most respectable buildings have at least one disgraceful way out. At the top she found a locked hatch, a broken skylight, and Cassian reaching down from the roof.","\"You are late,\" he said.","\"Your watch is judgmental.\"","He pulled her up. The movement brought them briefly chest to chest. She could hear the engine below and feel, absurdly, the steady beating of his heart. Then he took her hand and ran.","The roof sloped towards a timber walkway connecting the laboratory to an older building. Tessa and Ivo were already crossing it. Tessa carried the storage drive in her closed fist. Ivo had the manual under his arm and a look of profound dislike for every architect who had ever admired an exposed view.","Halfway across, the first board cracked.","\"Do not stop,\" he told himself aloud.","Mara recognized the effort in his voice. She kept her own light. \"If it helps, I have always considered you too irritating to fall gracefully.\"","\"It helps enormously.\"","They reached the far door. It opened onto a deserted room full of equipment crates and old promotional costumes. A row of painted angels stood on a shelf. Each held a model of the island's proposed research center. Several had lost their wings.","\"The marketing department had a vision,\" Mara said.","\"An unsustainable one,\" Cassian replied, looking at the dusty floor beneath them.","She found a loose grey dolman on a costume rack, the sort of old theatrical cloak whose broad sleeves could conceal half a person's arms. She threw it over her jacket and tied up her hair. It was not a disguise likely to survive close inspection. It might survive a glance through rain.","The rain arrived before she reached the window.","Beyond the dock, Rook's boat jerked against its ropes. The captain stood at the rail holding a telephone to her ear. Her free hand was waving them towards a lower path. Mara answered her own phone and heard three words before the connection broke.","\"Do. Not. Use—\"","A crash from the laboratory completed the sentence badly. The men had opened the hatch. Cassian braced the costume-room door while Ivo searched for another exit.","Tessa stared at the drive. \"The last group of files didn't transfer.\"","\"We have you,\" Mara said. \"We can work with what we have.\"","\"The original records are still—\"","\"We are not going back.\"","Mara heard the firmness in her own voice and understood, with some embarrassment, how she must sometimes sound to the people who tried to protect her. Tessa had not wanted adventure. She wanted eleven years of work to remain real.","\"I will help you preserve it,\" Mara said more quietly. \"But I cannot do that if we lose you with it.\"","Ivo found the exit behind a stack of battery casings. The narrow door led to a service passage, where a wheeled appliance blocked the path. Its price sticker was still attached. An expensive machine had become an obstacle because nobody had bothered to remove the packaging.","He gave it one indignant shove. It rolled into the wall.","\"I have improved the design,\" he announced.","The passage ended above a staircase cut into the rock. Mara went first. Halfway down, she could see the lower path Rook had indicated. She could also see why the captain had not wanted them to use the main steps. Water was pouring down them in a brown, violent stream.","They turned east along the cliff. The path was wide enough for one person, with a rusty rail on the ocean side. Cassian took the rear, giving Tessa the steadier place beside the rock. Rain struck Mara's borrowed cloak and ran from its hem. The fabric hid her hands, which was convenient when she needed to cling to something and inconvenient for almost everything else.","Above them, a window burst outward. A sharp smell drifted through the rain. Tessa looked up, horrified.","\"They've started a fire.\"","Smoke curled from the laboratory roof. The men had not merely abandoned the records. They were removing the possibility that anyone could compare the copies with their source.","Mara wanted to stop and photograph it. A wave struck the rocks below with enough force to shake the rail. She kept moving.","At the final bend, a section of the path had collapsed. The gap was short enough to jump and deep enough to make the choice unpleasant. Ivo stood on its near edge, staring at the water beyond.","Cassian stepped beside him. \"Look at me.\"","\"I am aware you are attractive. This seems a poor time.\"","Mara laughed once, helplessly. Even Tessa made a strangled sound.","\"The other side,\" Cassian said. \"One step forward. I will be behind you.\"","Ivo went. Tessa followed. Mara gathered the wet cloak and jumped, landing badly enough to feel the shock through her knee. She turned in time to see the rail tear loose behind Cassian.","He reached the far side. A jagged strip of metal caught his jacket and pulled him backward.","Mara caught his wrist with both hands.","For a second all she could see was the water beyond his shoulder. Then Ivo seized his other arm, Tessa caught Mara's coat, and their small, ungraceful coalition dragged him onto the path.","He lay still just long enough to terrify her.","\"Cassian.\"","\"Present,\" he said, breathless.","She hit his shoulder. Very lightly. It was either that or put her face against his neck and refuse to move.","\"Your jacket,\" she said.","He looked at the torn fabric. \"I was fond of it.\"","\"I will repair it if you stop trying to become part of the scenery.\"","His hand closed around hers. \"A compelling offer.\"","Rook met them at the lower landing. She had moved the boat away from the exposed dock, but the sheltered inlet was filling with water from the hill. A branch struck the hull, spun, and disappeared beneath it.","\"We leave now,\" she said.","Nobody proposed another opinion.","They climbed aboard. Ivo helped Tessa into the cabin, then turned back to check the rope. Mara thought he had forgotten to be afraid until she saw how carefully he kept his eyes above the water.","Rook started the motor. It coughed, caught, and died.","She tried again. The same sound, the same brief promise, the same silence.","The rain became a solid sheet beyond the windscreen. On the island, smoke rose through it, thick and grey. Tessa held the drive against her chest. Somewhere in that cloud, the main record of what had been done to Ewan and the other participants was burning.","Mara knelt beside Rook. \"What do you need?\"","\"Time. A clear fuel line. A less vindictive sea.\"","\"We can work on two of those.\"","The boat lifted as a larger wave entered the inlet. The rope drew taut with a sound like a string being tuned too far. Cassian reached for it. Mara caught his torn sleeve.","\"Together,\" she said.","He looked at her. For once he had no clever answer.","They braced the line as the wave struck."],"questions":[{"type":"推断","stem":"Ivo's behavior near the boat suggests that courage in this episode","options":["makes on the second wave.","For one astonishing moment nothing happened. The severed end fell into the water. Mara stared at it with the calm, stupid attention of a person waiting for the world to reconsider. Then the boat swung sideways towards the rock.","\"Down!\" Rook shouted.","Cassian pulled Mara against the cabin wall. Something struck the hull beneath them. The sound went through her bones. A cupboard opened, releasing a cup, three old charts, and a little wooden penguin that slid across the floor with greater dignity than anyone aboard possessed.","Ivo caught the tool case. Tessa caught the cupboard. Rook caught the wheel and announced that if anybody intended to pray, she hoped they would be specific.","\"To whom?\" Ivo asked.","\"Somebody with a working engine.\"","Mara laughed. The sound was too bright and entirely involuntary. She pressed a hand against the wall until it stopped shaking.","The boat was drifting out of the sheltered inlet. That was preferable to striking the cliff and considerably worse than remaining safely tied to it. Beyond the mouth of the inlet, the sea had lost every trace of its earlier grace. Waves rose in broken ridges. Rain turned the island into an indistinct grey mass, except where a golden pulse of fire shone through the laboratory windows.","\"Radio?\" Cassian asked.","\"Working,\" Rook said. \"Rescue knows where we are. They cannot produce a new vessel out of thin air.\"","\"Good. Then we only need to remain an old one.\"","He said it with such quiet certainty that Mara almost believed him. Then she saw the tear in his sleeve and the blood on the cloth underneath. He had caught the metal rail with more than his jacket.","She reached for him. He shook his head once.","\"In a minute.\"","She wanted to insist. Water struck the windscreen with a force that made the decision for her.","Ivo opened the engine cover. He had worked on electric motors, industrial pumps, manufacturing equipment, and a cinema projector whose operator had tried to lubricate it with cooking oil. None of those machines had been surrounded by this much moving water. His hands nevertheless became steadier when he had something mechanical to examine.","\"There,\" he said. \"The feed is obstructed.\"","Rook leaned over him. They exchanged a rapid series of questions Mara could not follow. She recognized what they were doing even when she did not understand the terms: each was checking an assumption before acting on it. Their expertise did not make the storm smaller. It made one small part of it manageable.","She took the radio when Rook handed it to her. A rescue operator asked for their position. Mara read the figures from the navigation display, repeated them when requested, and described the number of people aboard. The operator's calm questions gave her fear a useful shape.","Beside her, Tessa held the storage drive in both hands.","\"They will say I invented it,\" she whispered.","Mara looked at the small, sealed object. \"You are not the only person who has seen the files.\"","\"They will say you wanted to believe me.\"","\"Perhaps. They may say many things. We do not have to answer them here.\"","Tessa's eyes filled. She put the drive down at last and reached for the towel Mara offered. The movement seemed trivial. Mara knew it was not.","The engine turned once. Everyone went still. It turned again, caught with a harsh roar, and filled the little cabin with an oily, glorious noise.","\"You magnificent creature,\" Ivo told it.","\"I have been waiting years for him to say that to me,\" Mara said.","Rook took the wheel. The boat climbed the next wave instead of meeting it sideways. Mara felt her stomach rise and stay somewhere above her head. Cassian's hand found hers. She did not know which of them held more tightly.","For several minutes the world reduced itself to the bow, the water, and Rook's shoulders beneath her old coat. Then a warning light appeared beside the engine gauge. Ivo watched it without touching anything.","\"Temperature,\" he said.","The obstruction had not been the only fault. Rook reduced power. Their progress slowed to a hard, stubborn crawl. The island remained visible behind them, farther away but not yet far enough to feel harmless.","Across the radio, a voice announced that a larger rescue boat had left the eastern station. Its estimated arrival was fifteen minutes.","Fifteen minutes was not a lifetime. Mara understood, for the first time, how misleading that reassurance could be.","Something banged against the cabin door. A loose line had caught beneath the rail outside. Each time the boat rose, the line pulled against a damaged fitting. Rook could not leave the wheel. Ivo was watching the temperature. Tessa's wrist had swollen where the strap had cut it.","Cassian went to the door.","Mara stood with him. \"You are not doing that alone.\"","\"I was going to ask you to hold the door.\"","\"A very modest ambition.\"","He touched her cheek. The gesture was so brief that she could have imagined it if his hand had not been cold.","\"Mara. Whatever happens, I am very glad I met you.\"","\"You can say something more cheerful when we get home.\"","His mouth curved. \"I have been trying to think of a suitable occasion.\"","\"I shall arrange an outrageously expensive one.\"","\"Then I will have to survive it.\"","They opened the door together. Wind caught her borrowed cloak and dragged it sideways. She let the garment go. It spun into the rain like an injured bird and disappeared. Cassian clipped himself to the safety point Rook indicated, then moved low along the side of the cabin while Mara held the door clear.","The fitting had bent. He could not free the line with one hand. He looked back at her.","She understood before he spoke. He needed her nearer. He also needed the door held against the wind. It was the kind of small, impossible problem that could kill people while others argued about grand causes.","Mara took the loose end of the curtain cord and secured the handle to the inner rail. Then she went to him.","Together, they lifted the line from the bent fitting. A wave rose beside the boat. Cassian turned, putting his body between her and the water. It hit them both. For a moment she could see nothing. His arm held her against the cabin while the sea tore at her coat.","Then there was air. She drew it in with a sound she did not recognize as her own.","\"Inside,\" he said.","They staggered through the door. Ivo pulled it shut behind them. Cassian leaned against the wall, laughing once, breathlessly, as Mara stared at him with an anger too frightened to find words.","\"I know,\" he said.","She took his face in her wet hands. \"You do not get to make that sound and call it an explanation.\"","\"No.\"","For a second she thought he would say it now, whatever he had been saving for a suitable occasion. Instead he pressed his forehead against hers.","\"Home first,\" he whispered.","Through the rain ahead, a searchlight appeared. Rook swore with such feeling that the wooden penguin fell off the table again.","Mara closed her eyes. When she opened them, the light was still there.","She held Cassian's hand all the way towards it."],"questions":[{"type":"细节","stem":"Why does the group still have to manage the boat after contacting She accepted all three. Beside her, Ivo sat so still that the medic knelt to ask whether he was injured.","\"I am reconsidering several career decisions,\" he said.","\"That can wait until we reach the harbor.\"","\"Can it? I have a fresh appreciation of deadlines.\"","Tessa laughed into her cup. It was the first sound she had made since they climbed aboard the larger vessel. Her drive lay in a sealed bag between her boots. The rescue officer had recorded it among their possessions, along with a damaged telephone, two tool cases, and a watch whose owner insisted the scratch on its face was new.","Mara looked at Cassian. He raised one eyebrow.","\"You were the one who called it judgmental.\"","\"It survived the opportunity to improve.\"","His arm had been cleaned and bandaged. He was cold, exhausted, and noticeably less inclined to conceal either condition. She had never liked him more. The discovery was inconvenient enough that she spent several minutes staring at the harbor lights instead of his face.","Anika was waiting on the quay with Leah and Noor. Rook stepped down first and was embraced with such force that she complained about the structural integrity of her ribs. Ivo followed, looked briefly at the solid ground beneath him, and put his tool case down with almost ceremonial care.","Anika touched his shoulder. He turned towards her.","\"The engine ran,\" he said.","\"I heard.\"","\"Not perfectly.\"","\"I also heard that.\"","She hugged him. He looked so startled that Mara might have teased him if Noor had not been approaching with a face that ruled out entertainment.","\"Both of you,\" the doctor said, pointing at Mara and Cassian. \"Inside.\"","An hour later Mara was wrapped in dry clothes on a sofa in Vey House. Mrs. Wren had selected the clothes, the sofa, and a bowl of soup that apparently possessed the legal authority to prevent Mara from standing up. Cassian sat in the chair opposite, wearing a dark sweater. His wet hair had fallen over his forehead. He looked younger without the careful arrangement that usually made his face seem like a public institution.","\"Stop looking at me as if I might disappear,\" he said.","\"Then stop choosing interesting places to bleed.\"","Mrs. Wren entered with a second bowl. \"I shall leave you to settle that. If either of you faints, ring the bell before doing it.\"","She departed without waiting for agreement.","The fire made small, contented sounds in the grate. Mara ate because she knew Mrs. Wren would return and because it gave her something to do with her hands. The danger had passed. Her body had not accepted the news.","Cassian waited until she put down the spoon.","\"May I sit beside you?\"","She moved the blanket. He sat, leaving her enough room to choose the distance. She closed it at once, leaning against his uninjured side. The warmth of him was so ordinary and so astonishing that she nearly cried.","\"I had a plan,\" he said.","\"For the island? It needs revision.\"","\"For telling you something.\"","She lifted her head. His expression had become serious, but not in the way it became serious before a difficult interview. He looked like a man who had surrendered the protection of rehearsing.","\"It involved dinner,\" he continued. \"A respectable location. A day on which nobody had to climb anything.\"","\"An ambitious beginning.\"","\"I thought so. Then I realized I had already waited through an auction, a competition, a hospital opening, and a distressing amount of shipping.\"","Mara's laugh caught in her throat.","\"I love you,\" he said. \"I would prefer to say it without an emergency nearby. But I am no longer willing to let the absence of a perfect occasion become an excuse.\"","For a moment she could hear nothing except the fire.","She had been admired before. She had been wanted, underestimated, pursued, and occasionally offered a reasonable price for returning something valuable. None of those experiences had prepared her for the quiet steadiness of his face.","\"I am not particularly convenient,\" she said.","\"I have collected evidence.\"","\"I will argue.\"","\"Often.\"","\"I may steal your key again.\"","\"I have had another made.\"","She looked at him. \"For me?\"","\"For emergencies. You seem to attract them.\"","He took a small, plain key from the table and set it beside her bowl. It was not a demand that she move in, or a declaration that every part of his life now belonged to her. It was a door she could open when she wished.","That was what undid her.","\"I love you too,\" she said. \"I had intended to be much more impressive about it.\"","\"You dragged me away from a cliff. I have adjusted my standards.\"","She kissed him before he could improve the remark. His hand came to the back of her neck, warm and careful. There was nothing hurried in the way he kissed her back. After the sea, the alarms, the sound of something breaking under her feet, that unhurried certainty felt almost unbearable.","When she drew away, he was smiling.","\"You are looking intolerably pleased with yourself,\" she informed him.","\"The evidence is favorable.\"","She kissed him again to discourage further analysis.","They were still sitting close when Rowan opened the door without knocking, saw them, and stopped with one hand on the frame.","\"At last,\" he said.","Mara picked up a cushion. Cassian caught it before she could throw it at a man carrying hot tea.","\"I came bearing useful information,\" Rowan protested.","\"Then give it to the chair,\" she said.","He placed a folder on the chair and arranged the cups with an expression of wounded generosity. The harbor company had withdrawn its claim that the island evacuation had been orderly. Photographs taken by a fisherman showed the fire before the rescue boat arrived. That would not recover the destroyed workstation. It would make the simplest denial harder to sustain.","\"Tessa's files?\" Cassian asked.","\"Some readable, some incomplete. Ivo is working with an independent specialist tomorrow. Tonight he is under strict orders to sleep.\"","Mara looked at the folder. On top lay a copy of the demolition notice. The approval predated Tessa's call by six weeks. The site had been due for closure. Someone had chosen to use that closure as cover for something more immediate.","Below the notice was a delivery receipt bearing the same batch number they had traced at the hospital. The listed recipient was a water-testing station beneath the municipal conservatory.","The garden under the city, Mara thought.","Rowan followed her gaze. \"Tomorrow.\"","She opened her mouth.","\"Tomorrow,\" Cassian repeated.","She looked from one man to the other. \"I liked you better when you competed.\"","\"We have formed a coalition,\" Rowan said. \"It is proving unexpectedly effective.\"","He left them with the folder and the tea. Mara put the folder aside. Cassian held out his uninjured arm, and she settled against him again.","For once, the case could wait until morning.","When Mrs. Wren returned, she found the soup finished, the fire low, and the duke asleep with his cheek against the top of Mara's head. She drew another blanket over them and took the plain key off the table so it would not fall.","In the morning, she gave it to Mara without comment."],"questions":[{"type":"推断","stem":"Cassian placing Burn","volume":"卷四｜盐与钢的王冠","anchor":"chapter-0047","paragraphs":["June brought the morning papers in a shopping bag. She had bought every edition because their accounts differed in ways she found professionally offensive. In one, Mara had rescued a scientist. In another, she had stolen research property. In a third, she and Cassian had become engaged again, apparently during the storm.","\"You should have invited me,\" June said. \"I would have worn waterproof shoes.\"","Mara took the paper from her. The photograph showed Cassian carrying the tool case off the rescue boat while she stood behind him wrapped in a blanket. Somebody had drawn an arrow towards the blanket and identified it as a bridal shawl.","\"A bold interpretation of emergency equipment.\"","\"The readership enjoys romance.\"","Across the kitchen, Cassian placed a cup beside Mara without comment. June watched the easy way his hand rested briefly on Mara's shoulder.","\"Oh,\" she said.","\"If you scream,\" Mara warned her, \"Mrs. Wren will make you sort the linen.\"","June pressed both palms over her mouth. Her eyes did the screaming instead.","Ivo arrived before she recovered. He carried a padded case containing the circuit board Ewan had thrown from the tanker. Behind him came Dr. Elin Varr, a retired physicist who had known the Mercy House founders and had never mistaken an impressive brochure for a technical specification.","Varr examined the wedding photograph, then the bag of newspapers. \"If anyone is expecting Nobel prizes for this investigation, I recommend beginning with a reliable kettle.\"","\"The kettle works,\" Cassian said.","\"Excellent. We are ahead of the manufacturer.\"","They gathered around the long kitchen table. Mrs. Wren placed a cloth beneath the case before allowing it to touch the wood. Mara thought the intervention improved everyone's sense of proportion.","Tessa had arrived with Anika. Her wrist was bruised, but she had slept, eaten, and decided that fear was an inadequate reason to let Pell write the history of her work. She brought the original employment agreement and a folder of correspondence kept at home. The island's workstation had contained the full trial record. It had not contained every trace of the project's existence.","Varr put on her glasses. \"Tell me what you know. Then tell me which part you have only been told.\"","For once Mara did not resent the question. The physicist asked it like a person sorting tools, not a teacher laying a trap.","Ivo described the two versions of the circuit diagram. Tessa explained the changes to the trial heading, from fatigue to recall consistency. Anika identified the unauthorized cable. Mara showed them the flower's response to a nearby current. Cassian said very little until Varr asked about the alarm tone.","\"It brought back part of an earlier experience,\" he said. \"Not a complete memory.\"","Mara moved her foot against his beneath the table. He continued without looking at her.","Tessa had heard the same tone during an early demonstration. She found a reference in the correspondence: an audio cue introduced to help participants identify stages of a test. The company had later described the cue as a harmless feature of the apparatus. It was the kind of description that could be accurate in one setting and misleading in another.","Varr asked Noor to join them. The doctor arrived carrying a folder with so many colored tabs that Rowan respectfully moved his own papers aside.","\"No,\" Noor said before anyone asked her anything. \"I cannot tell you what the equipment does by looking at a chip.\"","\"A disappointment to the entire plot,\" June murmured.","Noor gave her a look. \"I can tell you that their report uses an assessment of mood as though it were a measure of memory. Those are different things.\"","One column classified several complaining participants as high in neuroticism. Another claimed improved function in the prefrontal cortex. The report supplied neither the observations necessary to justify the second claim nor a reason to treat the first as an explanation for equipment faults. Mara read the headings carefully. Scientific language could describe something precisely. It could also lend borrowed authority to an assumption.","\"Can we test the hardware without involving another person?\" she asked.","Varr's expression brightened. \"Now there is a useful question.\"","They did not perform the test in the kitchen. Mrs. Wren made that clear before anyone proposed it. Instead, Ivo and Varr took the board to the old workshop behind Mercy House, where an independent technician had agreed to document the procedure. Anika brought the published specifications. Tessa brought her own annotated copy.","Mara watched from behind a workbench. For the first time in days, she had nothing to steal, open, distract, or jump over. She found it almost intolerable.","Cassian noticed. \"You could rearrange the screws by their moral character.\"","\"The bent ones have been through enough.\"","\"The stainless steel?\"","\"Self-righteous.\"","He laughed, and the wait became easier.","The first result was ordinary. The board performed as the manufacturer said it should. The second test changed only the temperature range. A small indicator flickered. Ivo recorded it without touching the control. The same fault appeared in the third trial, then disappeared when the unit cooled.","Varr examined the measurements. She did not announce a breakthrough. She asked the technician to repeat the sequence with a different board from an intact instrument. They had only one suitable comparison unit. It was enough to identify a question worth pursuing, not enough to settle every question in the case.","Tessa looked at the display. \"That is what I reported.\"","The relief in her voice made Mara turn away for a moment. Tessa had spent months being told that her difficulty was personal, her judgment emotional, her observations inconvenient. A flickering light on a workbench did not give back those months. It gave her something smaller and indispensable: a fact another person could see.","Then Varr asked where the comparison unit had come from.","The technician checked the delivery slip. Municipal conservatory. Water quality monitoring. Same batch, different casing.","Noor stopped turning the pages of her folder.","\"If these instruments have been reporting inaccurate readings,\" she said, \"the apparent stability of the water supply may be an artifact of the equipment.\"","Mara looked at the two boards. She had expected a hidden device to reveal a secret by doing something extraordinary. Instead it had revealed one by failing at an ordinary job.","June closed her notebook. \"We need the independent water samples.\"","\"We need to find out whether any exist,\" Anika said.","Rowan took out his telephone. \"And I can find who paid for the replacement units.\"","The work divided itself among them with a speed that would once have made Mara afraid of losing control. Now it felt like room to breathe.","At the door, Tessa caught her hand.","\"I thought it was all gone.\"","Mara glanced back at the little board, the repeated measurements, the people arguing over what to test next.","\"They burned a room,\" she said. \"They did not get everyone who remembered working in it.\"","Outside, the sun had appeared between the clouds. Cassian was waiting beside the steps. He had bought her a paper bag of warm pastries from the high street and was trying to look as though it was an ordinary act, something any man might do for the woman who had stolen his keys and rearranged his life.","Mara took a pastry, stood on the lowest step, and kissed him in full view of the street.","From the workshop, June gave a muffled cry of triumph.","\"Linen,\" Mara called without turning.","The cry stopped."],"questions":[{"type":"论证结构","stem":"What is the main value of finding the same faulty component in a water-monitoring instrument?","options":["It raises a
